# Stage 2 implementation state

Updated: 2026-10-30. Baseline: `stage-2-2026-10-20-v2`.

## Current position

- Completed batches: `Batch-001` through `Batch-2496` (`dotj-core` E01 preparation, bounded WP-01/WP-02 E02/E03/N01 evidence, and internal foundation helpers). No batch is active.
- Internal Java ownership, type, context, cancellation, and async state sources plus executable fixtures exist in `dotj-core/dotj-core-01`; 16 source files build into an 18-class JDK-only JAR. The internal async helper includes timed waits and cleanup checks. The N01 selected-method ledger verifies 99 pinned public method signatures across nine async/cancellation types. No public .j compiler integration or qualified framework implementation exists. The C# oracles are reference tools.
- Core E01: **IN PROGRESS**. The source archive and notices are recorded in [source-archive-v10.0.12.json](repos/dotj-core/evidence/source-archive-v10.0.12.json). [Runtime artifact evidence](repos/dotj-core/evidence/runtime-artifact-v10.0.12-win-x64.json) confirms the official win-x64 ZIP checksum, 189 installed-runtime file matches, and identical CoreLib source subtrees between the embedded `dotnet/dotnet` commit and `dotnet/runtime` v10.0.12 tag. The pinned [oracle bootstrap manifest](repos/dotj-core/evidence/oracle-bootstrap-v10.0.12/manifest.json) records a clean C# build and run. The [bounded rights-scope record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/rights-scope-v10.0.12.json) verifies 14 selected C# file headers and direct generator inputs. The [Roslyn package record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/roslyn-package-v4.14.0/manifest.json) verifies the declared 4.14.0 NuGet artifact, MIT expression and notice. The [generator restore graph](repos/dotj-core/evidence/restore-graph-v10.0.12-netstandard2.0/manifest.json) confirms effective 4.14.0 and 20 resolved packages in one `netstandard2.0` profile with static graph disabled. A fresh [NuGet.org-only hash probe](repos/dotj-core/evidence/nuget-hash-probe-v4.14.0/manifest.json) reproduced the Azure-feed Roslyn hash difference. NuGet documents the metadata content hash as excluding package signature metadata, while the sidecar hashes the full signed package bytes. NuGet.org alone cannot restore the full generator project because required beta/internal packages are unavailable. Static-graph restore failed only when the redundant global `TargetFramework=netstandard2.0` property was passed; it succeeded without that property in a complete pinned extraction and the earlier subset. Transitive rights, file-to-notice applicability and generated-output release qualification remain open. This is not the mapped E03 behavior oracle; only the bounded CoreLib project build has passed, not a full upstream runtime build. See [baseline-lock.json](repos/dotj-core/evidence/baseline-lock.json).
- The [20-package rights inventory](repos/dotj-core/evidence/generator-package-rights-v10.0.12/manifest.json) snapshots every resolved package nuspec and 32 notice-like files. Fourteen nuspecs declare an MIT expression; six have legacy license URLs without a license element. One beta package has no notice-like extracted file by filename. This is an inventory, not rights clearance.
- The [complete-source static restore record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/full-source-static-restore-v10.0.12/manifest.json) verifies all 60,838 ZIP files are present after repairing three Windows extraction misses. With SDK 10.0.401, forced static restore passes without a global `TargetFramework` argument and fails with the redundant argument. The restored graph still has nine direct and 20 resolved packages; generator compilation passed in Batch-011.
- The [generator build record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/generator-build-v10.0.12/manifest.json) confirms the pinned generator project compiles with zero warnings and errors. Its source declares `ProductVersionInfo.g.cs`, class-derived EventSource hints and `NativeRuntimeEventSource.g.cs`. A downstream CoreLib restore passed, but the build failed with 60 `ILLink.Tasks` C# errors for missing MSBuild task types. No actual generated source files were verified in that batch.
- The [ILLink package-root record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/illink-package-root-v10.0.12/manifest.json) shows the Batch-011 task-reference errors came from building against the default NuGet cache while restore used an isolated package root. Passing `NuGetPackageRoot` to the isolated folder built `ILLink.Tasks` cleanly. The CoreLib build advanced to a Runtime Task Host error with an empty detected SDK version; generated source output was unverified at that stage.
- The [CoreLib task-host record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/corelib-taskhost-sdk-root-v10.0.12/manifest.json) shows `NetCoreSdkRoot` evaluated empty and the fallback RID graph path pointed into the repository, while `NETCoreSdkVersion` alone did not resolve the failure. Setting `NetCoreSdkRoot` to the installed SDK and preserving the isolated `NuGetPackageRoot` built pinned CoreLib successfully with zero warnings and errors. Generated files were still unverified in that batch; the full runtime build remains open.
- The [generated-source record](repos/dotj-core/evidence/corelib-generated-v10.0.12/manifest.json) captures eight generated CoreLib files (705,898 bytes) from that successful `windows.x64.Debug` build with individual SHA-256 hashes and generator input hashes. The generated `ProductVersionInfo.g.cs` reports `10.0.12` and `.NET 10.0.12-dev`; release-context generation and deterministic regeneration remain unverified. The full runtime build remains open.
- The [Release-configuration comparison](repos/dotj-core/evidence/corelib-release-generated-v10.0.12/manifest.json) records a zero-warning CoreLib Release build. Seven of eight generated outputs match the Debug bytes. `LibraryImports.g.cs` differs (4 added and 114 removed diff lines); compiler constants differ. `InformationalVersion` remains `10.0.12-dev`, so this is not an official release-artifact build. Deterministic regeneration and precise conditional-member attribution remain open.
- A [forced Release rebuild](repos/dotj-core/evidence/corelib-release-rebuild-v10.0.12/manifest.json) passed with zero warnings/errors and reproduced all eight generated-file hashes from the first Release build. This establishes repeatability in the same checkout, SDK and package cache; independent machine reproducibility and official release build provenance remain open.
- The [conditional import attribution](repos/dotj-core/evidence/corelib-conditional-imports-v10.0.12/manifest.json) maps all five `LibraryImports.g.cs` diff hunks to pinned source and evaluated properties: six generated methods are absent in Release because `FEATURE_JAVAMARSHAL` or `DEBUG` is off; two Windows interop methods use a different `SetLastErrorForDebug` value. This is source-condition evidence, not a semantic public API inventory or behavior oracle.

None directly establishes the designed WP-01 caller-ownership or cross-request context fixtures, and none has been executed.
- The [synchronous disposal oracle](repos/dotj-core/evidence/disposal-behavior-v10.0.12/manifest.json) builds and runs on hash-matched .NET 10.0.12 CoreLib. It records direct `IDisposable` dispatch, normal `using` cleanup, body/dispose failure ordering, and repeated direct calls. These are independent oracle observations, not the upstream xUnit suite or the designed caller-ownership/context fixtures.
- The [bounded disposal N01 ledger](repos/dotj-core/evidence/disposal-n01-v10.0.12.json) maps `System.IDisposable.Dispose(): void` to a proposed `System.IDisposable.Dispose()` Java spelling, with `AutoCloseable.close` only an optional adapter. `System.IAsyncDisposable.DisposeAsync(): ValueTask` is explicitly blocked pending the `ValueTask` contract; no public Java source or compiled API is claimed.
- The internal `OwnedResources` helper registers owned and borrowed `AutoCloseable` resources, deduplicates owned registrations by object identity, closes owned resources once in reverse order, and continues cleanup after a thrown `Exception` while preserving later failures as suppressed. The standalone Java test compiles and passes with JDK 21, including duplicate aliases and distinct resources with equal values. This is a partial WP-01-T2 implementation; no public facade or ownership/context fixture is qualified.

- Batch-700: Audit all 426 distinct declared-public methods in fourteen selected snapshots and the open qualification gates. Method inventory is bounded to selected snapshots; constructors, properties, public .j mapping, behavior and named acceptance gates remain open.
- Batch-701: Separate historical batch count, current 500-batch request, and outstanding acceptance items without a fabricated fixed total. Future total batches cannot be known while prerequisite and implementation work may require multiple reviewable batches; implementation remains 0/1,092.
- Batch-702: Add independently authored System.IDisposable product source and prove its exact declared public member with an external JDK-only consumer. This verifies one interface surface and dispatch only; ownership semantics, whole N01/E01 gates, and release qualification remain open.
- Batch-703: Add packaged-product IDisposable contract fixtures for exact shape, explicit dispatch, borrowed observation and failure propagation. The borrowed case is a consumer-level fixture, not a qualified product ownership implementation; N01/E01 and release gates remain open.
- Batch-704: Integrate public System.IDisposable into the internal owned-resource scope with identity-aware reverse cleanup and failure aggregation. The bridge is internal; whole WP-01 task, public facade, E01 rights and N01 qualification remain open.
- Batch-705: Add a bounded internal generic-arity map for the pinned Task, TaskCompletionSource and ValueTask pairs. This is an internal backend mapping only; no .j source binder, public Task/ValueTask implementation or N01 acceptance is qualified.
- Batch-706: Add and test a bounded Task-family type-reference binder that maps generic arity to distinct JVM names. The binder handles fully qualified references only and is not integrated into a .j compiler; public Task and N01 acceptance remain open.
- Batch-707: Bind Task-family arity in bounded fully qualified method return and parameter signatures while preserving member names. This parser is internal and does not compile .j source or qualify the public Task surface; E01-E03/N01 remain open.
- Batch-708: Demonstrate the java.lang.System source-name collision and a compiling import-based declaration using the bound Task arity names. Test-only placeholder Task types establish Java source feasibility, not Task behavior or a .j compiler; public N01 remains open.
- Batch-709: Add internal Java source rendering for arity-bound method signatures with deterministic System-type imports and collision rejection. This renders a bounded method declaration only; no .j compiler or public Task behavior is qualified.
- Batch-710: Reject Task-family imports that collide with names reserved by the generated declaration scope. The caller must supply its declaration-scope names; this is internal source rendering, not public .j compilation or N01 acceptance.
- Batch-711: Reject Java keyword and restricted-identifier spellings in bounded method and type references without adding a compiler-module dependency. This is a conservative Java 21 source subset; exact .NET symbols that need language escaping remain blocked in N01.
- Batch-712: Lower array-valued Task-family references, including generic arguments and method parameters, to distinct backend names. Only Java-style rectangular array suffixes are in this bounded parser; CLR multidimensional array identity and full .j compilation remain open.
- Batch-713: Bind Task-family arity recursively inside preserved generic container type references and method parameters. Outer generic names are preserved, not qualified as public .NET collection APIs; Task-family arity above one remains rejected.
- Batch-714: Bound generic type-reference parsing at 32 nested levels and 4096 source characters with deterministic errors. These are explicit internal parser limits, not qualified .NET type-system limits or public .j compiler behavior.
- Batch-715: Bound array-valued type references at 32 dimensions with a deterministic rejection at 33. This internal Java array syntax is not a CLR multidimensional-array or public .j compatibility claim.
- Batch-716: Bound method signatures at 8192 characters and 32 parameters, then validate the complete bounded foundation gate. The renderer remains internal; no .j compiler integration, public Task behavior or N01 acceptance is qualified.
